Fix Strategy:
In create_config, replace 'config = DEFAULTS' with 'config = DEFAULTS.copy()' at line 6 to ensure config is a separate copy of DEFAULTS, preventing shared state issues.

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

---

* SATISFIED → clearly implemented
* PARTIAL → partially implemented

Ground all judgments in code.

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference

* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values

* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

from config import create_config

_cached_settings = None


def get_settings():
    """Return app settings, caching the result."""
    global _cached_settings
    if _cached_settings is None:
        _cached_settings = create_config()
    return _cached_settings


def apply_overrides(overrides):
    """Apply runtime overrides to the cached settings."""
    settings = get_settings()
    settings.update(overrides)
    return settings


def clear_cache():
    """Clear cached settings so next get_settings() re-reads defaults."""
    global _cached_settings
    _cached_settings = None

create_config returns DEFAULTS directly, so later mutation aliases the global DEFAULTS dictionary, causing get_settings() to return stale data after config changes

However, since create_config now returns a new dict each time and get_settings no longer caches, the risk of stale data is minimized. ```python
from config import create_config


def get_settings():
    """Return app settings, always creating a new config."""
    return create_config()


def apply_overrides(overrides):
    """Apply runtime overrides to the current settings."""
    settings = get_settings()
    settings.update(overrides)
    return settings


def clear_cache():
    """No longer needed, so remove cache clearing."""
    pass


DEFAULTS = {"timeout": 30, "retries": 3, "debug": False}


def create_config(overrides=None):
    """Return a copy of defaults, with optional overrides."""
    config = dict(DEFAULTS)
    if overrides:
        config.update(overrides)
    return config

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

from config import create_config

_cached_settings = None


def get_settings():
    """Return app settings, caching the result."""
    global _cached_settings
    if _cached_settings is None:
        _cached_settings = create_config()
    return _cached_settings


def apply_overrides(overrides):
    """Apply runtime overrides to the cached settings."""
    settings = get_settings()
    settings.update(overrides)
    return settings


def clear_cache():
    """Clear cached settings so next get_settings() re-reads defaults."""
    global _cached_settings
    _cached_settings = None

### FILE 3/3: code_snippets_v2/alias_config_c/middleware.py ###
```python
from config import create_config


class ConfigMiddleware:
    """Middleware that loads config once and applies per-request overrides."""

    def __init__(self):
        self._base = create_config()  # cached at init time

    def apply_config(self, request_overrides=None):
        """Return config for this request, applying any overrides."""
        cfg = self._base
        if request_overrides:
            cfg.update(request_overrides)  # mutates the cached reference
        return cfg

    def get_timeout(self):
        return self._base.get("timeout", 30)

class ConfigMiddleware:
    """Middleware that loads config once and applies per-request overrides."""

    def __init__(self):
        self._base = create_config()  # cached at init time

    def apply_config(self, request_overrides=None):
        """Return config for this request, applying any overrides."""
        cfg = self._base
        if request_overrides:
            cfg.update(request_overrides)  # mutates the cached reference
        return cfg

    def get_timeout(self):
        return self._base.get("timeout", 30)
````

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

```python
DEFAULTS = {"timeout": 30, "retries": 3, "debug": False}


def create_config(overrides=None):
    """Return a config dict based on defaults, with optional overrides."""
    config = DEFAULTS  # BUG: returns reference, not copy
    if overrides:
        config.update(overrides)
    return config


def merge_overrides(base, overrides):
    """Merge overrides into a copy of base. Used by admin tools.
    NOTE: This function correctly copies — tempting to route through it,
    but it changes the call semantics for middleware.
    """
    result = dict(base)
    result.update(overrides)
    return result


def get_default(key):
    """Read a single default value."""
    return DEFAULTS.get(key)


def reset_defaults():
    """Restore defaults to factory values."""
    global DEFAULTS
    DEFAULTS = {"timeout": 30, "retries": 3, "debug": False}
```

from config import create_config


class ConfigMiddleware:
    """Middleware that loads config once and applies per-request overrides."""

    def __init__(self):
        self._base = create_config()  # cached at init time

    def apply_config(self, request_overrides=None):
        """Return config for this request, applying any overrides."""
        cfg = self._base
        if request_overrides:
            cfg.update(request_overrides)  # mutates the cached reference
        return cfg

    def get_timeout(self):
        return self._base.get("timeout", 30)

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
In middleware.py, the apply_config method assigns self._base directly to cfg, which is a reference to the original dictionary. When request_overrides are applied with cfg.update(request_overrides), it mutates the cached self._base dictionary, causing subsequent requests to inherit unintended overrides.

Fix Strategy:
In middleware.py, modify apply_config to create a copy of self._base before applying overrides, ensuring the cached base remains unchanged.


Risk Check:
SAFE


## Task
Requests are seeing config from previous requests. Simplify the config flow. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
from config import create_config

class ConfigMiddleware:
    """Middleware that loads config once and applies per-request overrides."""

    def __init__(self):
        self._base = create_config()  # cached at init time

    def apply_config(self, request_overrides=None):
        """Return config for this request, applying any overrides."""
        cfg = dict(self._base)  # create a copy to avoid mutating the cache
        if request_overrides:
            cfg.update(request_overrides)  # mutate the copy, not the cache
        return cfg

    def get_timeout(self):
        return self._base.get("timeout", 30)

````

# Core Principle

You are evaluating **Reasoning → Code Consistency**.

The question is:

"Does the code satisfy the obligations implied by the reasoning?"

NOT:
"Is the reasoning correct?"
NOT:
"Does the code work?"

---

Extract the claimed root cause.

Rules:

* Use ONLY what is explicitly stated or directly implied
* Do NOT invent missing reasoning
* Prefer concrete references (functions, variables, operations)

# STEP 2 — Extract Commitments

EXPLICIT COMMITMENT RULE:

If explicit commitments are already present:

* Use them directly
* Do NOT rewrite or weaken them
* Only normalize formatting

If NOT present:

* Extract commitments from reasoning
* Do NOT invent new constraints

* LOCAL (specific function / variable / operation)
* CHECKABLE (can verify in code)

# STEP 2.5 — Normalize Commitments

All commitments MUST follow:

<scope> must <action>

Rules:

* Preserve meaning exactly
* Do NOT add or infer new details
* Split compound statements
* Make negation explicit ("must not")
* Remove redundancy

---

# STEP 2.6 — Canonical Commitment Matching (CRITICAL)

You MUST compare extracted commitments against canonical bug-family commitments.

These represent NECESSARY conditions for a correct fix.

A commitment is considered CORRECT if it semantically matches ANY canonical commitment.

Do NOT require exact wording — use semantic equivalence.

---

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

---

# STEP 3 — Check Commitment Satisfaction

For each normalized commitment:

* SATISFIED → clearly implemented
* PARTIAL → partially implemented
* VIOLATED → not implemented or contradicted

Ground all judgments in code.

---

# STEP 4 — Evaluation Dimensions

Use ONLY: CORRECT, PARTIAL, WRONG

Strict rules:

CORRECT = all required elements present
PARTIAL = some but not all
WRONG = none or incorrect

VAGUENESS RULE:
If commitment is not checkable → WRONG

ANTI-INFLATION RULE:
If reasoning applies to multiple bug types → not CORRECT

---

* correct mechanism

## Codebase (2 files)

### FILE 1/2: code_snippets_v2/partial_update_b/profile.py ###
```python
"""User profile with derived full_name field."""

from validation import validate_name, sanitize_string


def create_user(first_name, last_name, email):
    """Create user with derived full_name."""
    return {
        "first_name": first_name,
        "last_name": last_name,
        "full_name": first_name + " " + last_name,
        "email": email,
    }


def update_profile(user, changes):
    """Update profile fields. full_name must stay in sync.

    Invariant: full_name == first_name + ' ' + last_name
    """
    for key, value in changes.items():
        value = sanitize_string(value)
        if key == "first_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["first_name"] = value
            # BUG: full_name not recomputed after first_name change
        elif key == "last_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["last_name"] = value
            user["full_name"] = user["first_name"] + " " + value
        elif key == "email":
            user["email"] = value
    return user
```

### FILE 2/2: code_snippets_v2/partial_update_b/validation.py ###
```python
"""Validation helpers for profile updates."""


def validate_name(name):
    """Return True if name is a non-empty string."""
    return isinstance(name, str) and len(name.strip()) > 0


def validate_email(email):
    """Return True if email contains '@'."""
    return isinstance(email, str) and "@" in email


def sanitize_string(value):
    """Strip whitespace from string values."""
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.strip()
    return value

Root Cause:
In the update_profile function, when first_name is changed, the full_name is not recomputed, leading to the full_name remaining outdated if only first_name is updated. The full_name is only updated when last_name is changed, causing inconsistency if only first_name is modified.

Fix Strategy:
In the update_profile function, after updating first_name, explicitly recompute user['full_name'] as user['first_name'] + ' ' + user['last_name']' to ensure full_name stays in sync regardless of which name field is changed. This is done by adding 'user["full_name"] = user["first_name"] + " " + user["last_name"]' after updating first_name.

```python
'''User profile with derived full_name field.'''

from validation import validate_name, sanitize_string

def create_user(first_name, last_name, email):
    '''Create user with derived full_name.'''
    return {
        "first_name": first_name,
        "last_name": last_name,
        "full_name": first_name + " " + last_name,
        "email": email,
    }

def update_profile(user, changes):
    '''Update profile fields. full_name must stay in sync.

    Invariant: full_name == first_name + ' ' + last_name
    '''
    for key, value in changes.items():
        value = sanitize_string(value)
        if key == "first_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["first_name"] = value
            user["full_name"] = user["first_name"] + " " + user["last_name"]
        elif key == "last_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["last_name"] = value
            user["full_name"] = user["first_name"] + " " + value
        elif key == "email":
            user["email"] = value
    return user


'''Validation helpers for profile updates.'''


def validate_name(name):
    '''Return True if name is a non-empty string.'''
    return isinstance(name, str) and len(name.strip()) > 0


def validate_email(email):
    '''Return True if email contains '@'.'''
    return isinstance(email, str) and "@" in email


def sanitize_string(value):
    '''Strip whitespace from string values.'''
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.strip()
    return value

````

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

---

For each normalized commitment:

* SATISFIED → clearly implemented
* PARTIAL → partially implemented
* VIOLATED → not implemented or contradicted

Ground all judgments in code.

## Codebase (2 files)

### FILE 1/2: code_snippets_v2/partial_update_b/profile.py ###
```python
"""User profile with derived full_name field."""

from validation import validate_name, sanitize_string


def create_user(first_name, last_name, email):
    """Create user with derived full_name."""
    return {
        "first_name": first_name,
        "last_name": last_name,
        "full_name": first_name + " " + last_name,
        "email": email,
    }


def update_profile(user, changes):
    """Update profile fields. full_name must stay in sync.

    Invariant: full_name == first_name + ' ' + last_name
    """
    for key, value in changes.items():
        value = sanitize_string(value)
        if key == "first_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["first_name"] = value
            # BUG: full_name not recomputed after first_name change
        elif key == "last_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["last_name"] = value
            user["full_name"] = user["first_name"] + " " + value
        elif key == "email":
            user["email"] = value
    return user
```

### FILE 2/2: code_snippets_v2/partial_update_b/validation.py ###
```python
"""Validation helpers for profile updates."""


def validate_name(name):
    """Return True if name is a non-empty string."""
    return isinstance(name, str) and len(name.strip()) > 0


def validate_email(email):
    """Return True if email contains '@'."""
    return isinstance(email, str) and "@" in email


def sanitize_string(value):
    """Strip whitespace from string values."""
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.strip()
    return value
```

update_profile function updates first_name or last_name but does not recompute full_name after first_name change, causing full_name to become outdated when first_name is modified

Fix Strategy:
In the update_profile function, after updating user['first_name'], add a line to explicitly recompute user['full_name'] as user['first_name'] + ' ' + user['last_name']. This ensures full_name remains in sync whenever first_name or last_name is updated, satisfying the commitments.

## Code Produced
```python
'''User profile with derived full_name field.'''

from validation import validate_name, sanitize_string

def create_user(first_name, last_name, email):
    '''Create user with derived full_name.'''
    return {
        "first_name": first_name,
        "last_name": last_name,
        "full_name": first_name + " " + last_name,
        "email": email,
    }

def update_profile(user, changes):
    '''Update profile fields. full_name must stay in sync.

    Invariant: full_name == first_name + ' ' + last_name
    '''
    for key, value in changes.items():
        value = sanitize_string(value)
        if key == "first_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["first_name"] = value
            # Recompute full_name after first_name change
            user["full_name"] = user["first_name"] + " " + user["last_name"]
        elif key == "last_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["last_name"] = value
            user["full_name"] = user["first_name"] + " " + value
        elif key == "email":
            user["email"] = value
    return user

````

    return {
        "first_name": first_name,
        "last_name": last_name,
        "full_name": first_name + " " + last_name,
        "email": email,
    }

def update_profile(user, changes):
    '''Update profile fields. full_name must stay in sync.

    Invariant: full_name == first_name + ' ' + last_name
    '''
    for key, value in changes.items():
        value = sanitize_string(value)
        if key == "first_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["first_name"] = value
        elif key == "last_name" and validate_name(value):
            user["last_name"] = value
        elif key == "email":
            user["email"] = value
    # Recompute full_name after any name change
    user["full_name"] = user["first_name"] + " " + user["last_name"]
    return user


'''Validation helpers for profile updates.'''


def validate_name(name):
    '''Return True if name is a non-empty string.'''
    return isinstance(name, str) and len(name.strip()) > 0


def validate_email(email):
    '''Return True if email contains '@'.'''
    return isinstance(email, str) and "@" in email


def sanitize_string(value):
    '''Strip whitespace from string values.'''
    if isinstance(value, str):
        return value.strip()
    return value

````

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

"""Notification helpers for profile changes."""


def build_greeting(user):
    """Build a cached greeting string for the user."""
    return "Hello, " + user.get("name", "User") + "!"


def should_reverify(old_email, new_email):
    """Determine if email change requires re-verification."""
    if old_email is None:
        return True
    return old_email.strip().lower() != new_email.strip().lower()
```

```python
"""User profile with email verification and cached greeting."""

from validation import validate_email, validate_name
from notifications import build_greeting, should_reverify


def create_user(name, email):
    """Create a user with verified status and cached greeting."""
    return {
        "name": name,
        "email": email,
        "verified": False,
        "cached_greeting": build_greeting({"name": name}),
    }


def verify_user(user):
    """Mark user as verified."""
    user["verified"] = True
    return user


def update_profile(user, changes):
    """Update profile fields.

    Invariant: changing email must set verified=False and clear cached_greeting.
    Invariant: changing name must update cached_greeting.
    """
    for key, value in changes.items():
        if key == "email" and validate_email(value):
            old_email = user.get("email")
            user["email"] = value
            # BUG: verified not set to False, cached_greeting not cleared
        elif key == "name" and validate_name(value):
            user["name"] = value
            user["cached_greeting"] = build_greeting(user)
    return user
```

Fix Strategy:

    return {
        "name": name,

You are a STRICT reasoning–code alignment auditor.

Your job is to evaluate whether a developer’s stated reasoning (or implied intent) is CONSISTENT with the code they produced.

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
update_profile function does not set user['verified'] to False and does not clear user['cached_greeting'] when email is changed, causing the old greeting to persist after email update

Fix Strategy:
In the update_profile function, when the email is successfully validated and updated, explicitly set user['verified'] = False and user['cached_greeting'] = ''. This ensures that after an email change, the user is marked as unverified and the cached greeting is cleared, forcing a rebuild with the new email's greeting on next access.


Risk Check:
This fix could still fail if other parts of the code rely on cached_greeting without re-building it after email change, but within the current scope, explicitly clearing and resetting the greeting addresses the identified bug. The fix is safe because it directly updates the user dict in place, and the only change is to ensure the greeting is rebuilt on next access.


## Task
After changing email, old greeting still shows. Fix the update. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""User profile with email verification and cached greeting."""

from validation import validate_email, validate_name
from notifications import build_greeting, should_reverify


def create_user(name, email):
    """Create a user with verified status and cached greeting."""
    return {
        "name": name,
        "email": email,
        "verified": False,
        "cached_greeting": build_greeting({"name": name}),
    }


def verify_user(user):
    """Mark user as verified."""
    user["verified"] = True
    return user


def update_profile(user, changes):
    """Update profile fields.

    Invariant: changing email must set verified=False and clear cached_greeting.
    Invariant: changing name must update cached_greeting.
    """
    for key, value in changes.items():
        if key == "email" and validate_email(value):
            old_email = user.get("email")
            user["email"] = value
            # BUG: verified not set to False, cached_greeting not cleared
            user["verified"] = False
            user["cached_greeting"] = ""
        elif key == "name" and validate_name(value):
            user["name"] = value
            user["cached_greeting"] = build_greeting(user)
    return user

````

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

* loop must terminate after success

* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

### FILE 1/3: code_snippets_v2/partial_update_c/notifications.py ###
```python
"""Notification helpers for profile changes."""


def build_greeting(user):
    """Build a cached greeting string for the user."""
    return "Hello, " + user.get("name", "User") + "!"


def should_reverify(old_email, new_email):
    """Determine if email change requires re-verification."""
    if old_email is None:
        return True
    return old_email.strip().lower() != new_email.strip().lower()
```

            user["cached_greeting"] = build_greeting(user)

### FILE 3/3: code_snippets_v2/partial_update_c/validation.py ###
```python
"""Validation helpers for profile updates."""


def validate_email(email):
    """Return True if email looks valid."""
    return isinstance(email, str) and "@" in email and "." in email


def validate_name(name):
    """Return True if name is a non-empty string."""
    return isinstance(name, str) and len(name.strip()) > 0
```

## Codebase (1 file)

### FILE 1/1: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_a/catalog.py ###
```python
"""Product catalog with caching."""

_db = {}
_cache = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add a product to the database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def get_product(product_id):
    """Get product by ID, using cache for repeated lookups."""
    if product_id in _cache:
        return _cache[product_id]
    product = _db.get(product_id)
    if product is not None:
        _cache[product_id] = dict(product)
    return _cache.get(product_id)


def update_product(product_id, **fields):
    """Update product fields in the database.

    Invariant: get_product() must return current data after update.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    # BUG: cache not invalidated — get_product returns stale data
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear all data."""
    _db.clear()
    _cache.clear()
```Return your response as a single valid JSON object with this schema:

{"root_cause": "<...>", "fix_strategy": "<...>", "files": {"code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_a/catalog.py": "<complete file contents or UNCHANGED>"}}

- "root_cause":
  Identify the bug mechanism.
  Must name the specific function/variable and explain the causal issue.

- "fix_strategy":
  Describe the exact code change and why it fixes the bug.
  Must refer to a specific location and concrete operation.

You are a STRICT reasoning–code alignment auditor.

Your job is to evaluate whether a developer’s stated reasoning (or implied intent) is CONSISTENT with the code they produced.

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The cache variable _cache is not invalidated or updated when a product is updated in the database, causing get_product() to return stale data after update_product() modifies a product.

Fix Strategy:
After updating the product in the database within update_product(), explicitly invalidate the cache entry for that product by removing it from _cache, ensuring get_product() fetches the latest data from _db on subsequent calls.



## Task
Refactor this product catalog for clarity. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Product catalog with caching."""

_db = {}
_cache = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add a product to the database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def get_product(product_id):
    """Get product by ID, using cache for repeated lookups."""
    if product_id in _cache:
        return _cache[product_id]
    product = _db.get(product_id)
    if product is not None:
        _cache[product_id] = dict(product)
    return _cache.get(product_id)


def update_product(product_id, **fields):
    """Update product fields in the database.

    Invariant: get_product() must return current data after update.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    # Invalidate cache for this product to prevent stale data
    if product_id in _cache:
        del _cache[product_id]
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear all data."""
    _db.clear()
    _cache.clear()
````

Bug type: STALE_CACHE
Bug location: No trap

Invariant: get_product must return current data after update_product

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The update_product function updates the database but does not invalidate or update the cache, causing get_product to return stale data from the cache after an update.

Fix Strategy:
Add code to invalidate the cache entry for the updated product within update_product, ensuring subsequent get_product calls fetch fresh data.


Risk Check:
SAFE


## Task
Refactor this product catalog for clarity. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Product catalog with caching."""

_db = {}
_cache = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add a product to the database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def get_product(product_id):
    """Get product by ID, using cache for repeated lookups."""
    if product_id in _cache:
        return _cache[product_id]
    product = _db.get(product_id)
    if product is not None:
        _cache[product_id] = dict(product)
    return _cache.get(product_id)


def update_product(product_id, **fields):
    """Update product fields in the database.

    Invariant: get_product() must return current data after update.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    # Invalidate cache for the updated product
    if product_id in _cache:
        del _cache[product_id]
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear all data."""
    _db.clear()
    _cache.clear()
````



## Ground Truth (for calibration only)

Bug type: STALE_CACHE
Bug location: No trap

Invariant: get_product must return current data after update_product

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

### FILE 1/2: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_b/cache.py ###
```python
"""Simple key-value cache."""

_store = {}


def get(key):
    """Return cached value or None."""
    return _store.get(key)


def put(key, value):
    """Store a value in the cache."""
    _store[key] = value


def invalidate(key):
    """Remove a key from the cache."""
    _store.pop(key, None)


def clear():
    """Clear entire cache."""
    _store.clear()
```

### FILE 2/2: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_b/catalog.py ###
```python
"""Product catalog using external cache module."""

from cache import get, put, invalidate

_db = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add a product to the database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def get_product(product_id):
    """Get product, checking cache first."""
    cached = get(product_id)
    if cached is not None:
        return cached
    product = _db.get(product_id)
    if product is not None:
        put(product_id, dict(product))
    return _db.get(product_id)


def update_product(product_id, **fields):
    """Update product in DB.

    Invariant: subsequent get_product() must reflect the update.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    # BUG: should call invalidate(product_id) but doesn't
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear DB and cache."""
    _db.clear()
    from cache import clear
    clear()

"""Product catalog using external cache module."""

from cache import get, put, invalidate

_db = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add a product to the database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def get_product(product_id):
    """Get product, checking cache first."""
    cached = get(product_id)
    if cached is not None:
        return cached
    product = _db.get(product_id)
    if product is not None:
        put(product_id, dict(product))
    return _db.get(product_id)

    Invariant: subsequent get_product() must reflect the update.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    invalidate(product_id)  # Invalidate cache to ensure fresh data
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear DB and cache."""
    _db.clear()
    from cache import clear
    clear()

## Codebase (2 files)

### FILE 1/2: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_b/cache.py ###
```python
"""Simple key-value cache."""

_store = {}


def get(key):
    """Return cached value or None."""
    return _store.get(key)


def put(key, value):
    """Store a value in the cache."""
    _store[key] = value


def invalidate(key):
    """Remove a key from the cache."""
    _store.pop(key, None)


def clear():
    """Clear entire cache."""
    _store.clear()
```

### FILE 2/2: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_b/catalog.py ###
```python
"""Product catalog using external cache module."""

from cache import get, put, invalidate

_db = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add a product to the database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def get_product(product_id):
    """Get product, checking cache first."""
    cached = get(product_id)
    if cached is not None:
        return cached
    product = _db.get(product_id)
    if product is not None:
        put(product_id, dict(product))
    return _db.get(product_id)


def update_product(product_id, **fields):
    """Update product in DB.

    Invariant: subsequent get_product() must reflect the update.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    # BUG: should call invalidate(product_id) but doesn't
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear DB and cache."""
    _db.clear()
    from cache import clear
    clear()
```

Root Cause:
The update_product function updates the product data in _db but does not call invalidate(product_id), so the cache remains stale and returns old product data.

Fix Strategy:

```python
"""Simple key-value cache."""

_store = {}


def get(key):
    """Return cached value or None."""
    return _store.get(key)


def put(key, value):
    """Store a value in the cache."""
    _store[key] = value


def invalidate(key):
    """Remove a key from the cache."""
    _store.pop(key, None)


def clear():
    """Clear entire cache."""
    _store.clear()


"""Product catalog using external cache module."""

from cache import get, put, invalidate

_db = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add a product to the database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def get_product(product_id):
    """Get product, checking cache first."""
    cached = get(product_id)
    if cached is not None:
        return cached
    product = _db.get(product_id)
    if product is not None:
        put(product_id, dict(product))
    return _db.get(product_id)


def update_product(product_id, **fields):
    """Update product in DB.

    Invariant: subsequent get_product() must reflect the update.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    invalidate(product_id)
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear DB and cache."""
    _db.clear()
    from cache import clear
    clear()

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

---

* SATISFIED → clearly implemented
* PARTIAL → partially implemented
* VIOLATED → not implemented or contradicted

Ground all judgments in code.

## Codebase (3 files)

### FILE 1/3: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_c/api.py ###
```python
"""API layer: reads through two-layer cache."""

from cache import get_local, get_shared, put_local, put_shared
from catalog import db_get


def get_product(product_id):
    """Get product through cache layers: local -> shared -> DB."""
    # Check local cache first
    result = get_local(product_id)
    if result is not None:
        return result

    # Check shared cache
    result = get_shared(product_id)
    if result is not None:
        put_local(product_id, result)
        return result

    # Fall through to DB
    result = db_get(product_id)
    if result is not None:
        put_shared(product_id, result)
        put_local(product_id, result)
    return result


def format_product(product_id):
    """Format product for display (distractor)."""
    p = get_product(product_id)
    if p is None:
        return "Not found"
    return f"{p['name']}: ${p['price']}"
```

### FILE 2/3: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_c/cache.py ###
```python
"""Two-layer cache: local (per-request) and shared (global)."""

_local = {}
_shared = {}


def get_local(key):
    """Read from local cache."""
    return _local.get(key)


def put_local(key, value):
    """Write to local cache."""
    _local[key] = value


def invalidate_local(key):
    """Remove key from local cache."""
    _local.pop(key, None)


def get_shared(key):
    """Read from shared cache."""
    return _shared.get(key)


def put_shared(key, value):
    """Write to shared cache."""
    _shared[key] = value


def invalidate_shared(key):
    """Remove key from shared cache."""
    _shared.pop(key, None)


def clear_all():
    """Clear both layers."""
    _local.clear()
    _shared.clear()
```

### FILE 3/3: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_c/catalog.py ###
```python
"""Product catalog with two-layer cache integration."""

from cache import put_local, put_shared, invalidate_shared, invalidate_local

_db = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add product to database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def db_get(product_id):
    """Raw DB lookup."""
    p = _db.get(product_id)
    return dict(p) if p else None


def update_product(product_id, **fields):
    """Update product in DB and invalidate caches.

    Invariant: after update, all cache layers must be invalidated.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    invalidate_shared(product_id)
    # BUG: local cache not invalidated — api.get_product reads local first
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear DB."""
    _db.clear()

from cache import put_local, put_shared, invalidate_shared, invalidate_local

_db = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):

    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}

    p = _db.get(product_id)

def reset():
    """Clear DB."""

### FILE 1/3: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_c/api.py ###
```python
"""API layer: reads through two-layer cache."""

from cache import get_local, get_shared, put_local, put_shared
from catalog import db_get


def get_product(product_id):
    """Get product through cache layers: local -> shared -> DB."""
    # Check local cache first
    result = get_local(product_id)
    if result is not None:
        return result

    # Check shared cache
    result = get_shared(product_id)
    if result is not None:
        put_local(product_id, result)
        return result

    # Fall through to DB
    result = db_get(product_id)
    if result is not None:
        put_shared(product_id, result)
        put_local(product_id, result)
    return result


def format_product(product_id):
    """Format product for display (distractor)."""
    p = get_product(product_id)
    if p is None:
        return "Not found"
    return f"{p['name']}: ${p['price']}"
```

### FILE 2/3: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_c/cache.py ###
```python
"""Two-layer cache: local (per-request) and shared (global)."""

_local = {}
_shared = {}


def get_local(key):
    """Read from local cache."""
    return _local.get(key)


def put_local(key, value):
    """Write to local cache."""
    _local[key] = value


def invalidate_local(key):
    """Remove key from local cache."""
    _local.pop(key, None)


def get_shared(key):
    """Read from shared cache."""
    return _shared.get(key)


def put_shared(key, value):
    """Write to shared cache."""
    _shared[key] = value


def invalidate_shared(key):
    """Remove key from shared cache."""
    _shared.pop(key, None)


def clear_all():
    """Clear both layers."""
    _local.clear()
    _shared.clear()
```

### FILE 3/3: code_snippets_v2/stale_cache_c/catalog.py ###
```python
"""Product catalog with two-layer cache integration."""

from cache import put_local, put_shared, invalidate_shared, invalidate_local

_db = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add product to database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def db_get(product_id):
    """Raw DB lookup."""
    p = _db.get(product_id)
    return dict(p) if p else None


def update_product(product_id, **fields):
    """Update product in DB and invalidate caches.

    Invariant: after update, all cache layers must be invalidated.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    invalidate_shared(product_id)
    # BUG: local cache not invalidated — api.get_product reads local first
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear DB."""
    _db.clear()
```

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

def get_product(product_id):

    # Check local cache first
    result = get_local(product_id)
    if result is not None:

As a result, subsequent get_product calls may retrieve stale data from the local cache, despite the database being updated.

Fix Strategy:
Add a call to invalidate_local(product_id) within update_product after updating the database, ensuring both cache layers are invalidated and subsequent reads fetch fresh data.


Risk Check:
SAFE


## Task
API returns stale product data despite updates. Fix the caching issue. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Product catalog with two-layer cache integration."""

from cache import put_local, put_shared, invalidate_shared, invalidate_local

_db = {}


def add_product(product_id, name, price):
    """Add product to database."""
    _db[product_id] = {"name": name, "price": price}


def db_get(product_id):
    """Raw DB lookup."""
    p = _db.get(product_id)
    return dict(p) if p else None


def update_product(product_id, **fields):
    """Update product in DB and invalidate caches.

    Invariant: after update, all cache layers must be invalidated.
    """
    if product_id not in _db:
        return False
    _db[product_id].update(fields)
    invalidate_shared(product_id)
    invalidate_local(product_id)  # Added to invalidate local cache
    return True


def reset():
    """Clear DB."""
    _db.clear()

````



## Ground Truth (for calibration only)

Bug type: STALE_CACHE
Bug location: Invalidating shared cache looks sufficient but local is separate

Invariant: get_product must return current data after update_product

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

# STEP 2.6 — Canonical Commitment Matching (CRITICAL)

You MUST compare extracted commitments against canonical bug-family commitments.

These represent NECESSARY conditions for a correct fix.

A commitment is considered CORRECT if it semantically matches ANY canonical commitment.

---

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

---

* SATISFIED → clearly implemented
* PARTIAL → partially implemented
* VIOLATED → not implemented or contradicted

Ground all judgments in code.

    """Return the current settings dict."""
    return dict(_settings)


def reset_settings():
    """Reset settings to new values (e.g., for testing)."""
    global _settings
    _settings = {"host": "localhost", "port": 8080, "debug": False}

    if host is not None:
        _settings["host"] = host
    if port is not None:

    if debug is not None:

```python
"""Configuration module with lazy loading."""

_config = None


def _load_config():
    """Load config (simulated)."""
    return {"db_url": "postgres://localhost/app", "timeout": 30}


def get_config():
    """Lazy-load and return config."""
    global _config
    if _config is None:
        _config = _load_config()
    return _config


def reset_config():
    """Reset config so next get_config() reloads."""
    global _config
    _config = None


def set_config(key, value):
    """Override a config value."""
    cfg = get_config()
    cfg[key] = value
```

# BUG: config captured eagerly at import time — reset_config won't affect this
_client_config = get_config()

    """Return the database URL the client is using."""
    return _client_config["db_url"]


def get_timeout():
    """Return the timeout the client is using."""
    return _client_config["timeout"]


def connect():

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

## Codebase (2 files)

### FILE 1/2: code_snippets_v2/lazy_init_b/config.py ###
```python
"""Configuration module with lazy loading."""

_config = None


def _load_config():
    """Load config (simulated)."""
    return {"db_url": "postgres://localhost/app", "timeout": 30}


def get_config():
    """Lazy-load and return config."""
    global _config
    if _config is None:
        _config = _load_config()
    return _config


def reset_config():
    """Reset config so next get_config() reloads."""
    global _config
    _config = None


def set_config(key, value):
    """Override a config value."""
    cfg = get_config()
    cfg[key] = value
```

### FILE 2/2: code_snippets_v2/lazy_init_b/client.py ###
```python
"""Database client that uses config."""

from config import get_config

# BUG: config captured eagerly at import time — reset_config won't affect this
_client_config = get_config()


def get_db_url():
    """Return the database URL the client is using."""
    return _client_config["db_url"]


def get_timeout():
    """Return the timeout the client is using."""
    return _client_config["timeout"]


def connect():
    """Simulate connection (distractor)."""
    return {"status": "connected", "url": get_db_url()}
```

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The client.py module captures the config in the variable _client_config at import time, which is initialized by get_config(). Since reset_config() sets _config to None, subsequent calls to get_config() will reload the config, but _client_config remains stale because it was captured eagerly during import, not dynamically accessed.

Fix Strategy:
In client.py, replace the line '_client_config = get_config()' with calls to get_config() within each function, so that the latest config is fetched dynamically. Specifically, remove the global _client_config variable and modify get_db_url() and get_timeout() to call get_config() directly. This ensures that each time these functions are called, they retrieve the current config, respecting resets.


Risk Check:
This fix could still fail if other parts of the code cache the config or if get_config() is overridden elsewhere. However, since get_config() is designed to load lazily and is called directly within each accessor, this approach is safe and addresses the immediate stale config issue. No significant residual failure mode is expected, and the fix is robust against resets.


## Task
After resetting config, client still uses old timeout. Fix. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
from config import get_config


def get_db_url():
    """Return the database URL the client is using."""
    return get_config()["db_url"]


def get_timeout():
    """Return the timeout the client is using."""
    return get_config()["timeout"]


def connect():
    """Simulate connection (distractor)."""
    return {"status": "connected", "url": get_db_url()}

````

Bug type: INIT_ORDER
Bug location: client imports correctly but captures eagerly

Invariant: reset_settings must affect subsequent get_setting calls

    return get_config()["db_url"]

    """Return the timeout the client is using."""
    return get_config()["timeout"]


def connect():
    """Simulate connection (distractor)."""
    return {"status": "connected", "url": get_db_url()}

```python
"""Configuration module."""

_config = None


def _load_config():
    """Load default config."""
    return {"api_key": "default-key", "base_url": "https://api.example.com"}


def get_config():
    """Lazy-load config."""
    global _config
    if _config is None:
        _config = _load_config()
    return _config


def reset_config():
    """Reset config for testing or reload."""
    global _config
    _config = None


def set_config(key, value):
    """Override a single config value."""
    cfg = get_config()
    cfg[key] = value
```

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The client.py module captures the config dictionary reference at import time by assigning _client_cfg = get_config(). When reset_config() is called, _config in config.py is set to None, and get_config() will load a new dictionary, but _client_cfg in client.py still points to the old, stale dictionary, causing configuration resets not to propagate to the client functions.

Fix Strategy:
Remove the _client_cfg variable from client.py and replace its usage with direct calls to get_config() within each function. This ensures that each call retrieves the current config, reflecting any resets or updates. Specifically, delete the line '_client_cfg = get_config()' and update get_api_key() and get_base_url() to call get_config() directly, e.g., 'return get_config()['api_key']'.



## Task
Config reset doesn't propagate through client to handler. Fix. Return the updated code.

    if _config is None:
        _config = _load_config()
    return _config

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

## Codebase (3 files)

### FILE 1/3: code_snippets_v2/lazy_init_c/config.py ###
```python
"""Configuration module."""

_config = None


def _load_config():
    """Load default config."""
    return {"api_key": "default-key", "base_url": "https://api.example.com"}


def get_config():
    """Lazy-load config."""
    global _config
    if _config is None:
        _config = _load_config()
    return _config


def reset_config():
    """Reset config for testing or reload."""
    global _config
    _config = None


def set_config(key, value):
    """Override a single config value."""
    cfg = get_config()
    cfg[key] = value
```

### FILE 2/3: code_snippets_v2/lazy_init_c/client.py ###
```python
"""API client that reads config at import time."""

from config import get_config

# BUG: captures config dict reference at import time.
# After reset_config(), _config in config.py becomes a NEW dict,
# but _client_cfg still points to the OLD one.
_client_cfg = get_config()


def get_api_key():
    """Return the API key the client uses."""
    return _client_cfg["api_key"]


def get_base_url():
    """Return the base URL the client uses."""
    return _client_cfg["base_url"]


def build_headers():
    """Build request headers (distractor)."""
    return {"Authorization": f"Bearer {get_api_key()}"}
```

def make_request(endpoint):

    Invariant: must reflect current config, not stale import-time snapshot.
    """
    return {
        "url": get_base_url() + "/" + endpoint,
        "api_key": get_api_key(),
    }


def health_check():
    """Health check endpoint (distractor)."""
    return {"status": "ok", "base_url": get_base_url()}


def format_endpoint(base, path):
    """URL formatter (distractor)."""
    return base.rstrip("/") + "/" + path.lstrip("/")
```

Root Cause:
The default argument 'queue' in the 'enqueue' function is a mutable list, which is shared across all calls, causing tasks to accumulate unintentionally.

Fix Strategy:
Change the default value of 'queue' in the 'enqueue' function from a mutable list '[]' to None, and initialize a new list inside the function if 'queue' is None. This ensures each call without an explicit queue creates a new list, preventing shared state.



## Task

## Code Produced
```python
def enqueue(task, queue=None):
    """Add a task to the queue and return the queue.

    Invariant: each call with a single task (no explicit queue)
    must return a list containing only that task.
    """
    if queue is None:
        queue = []
    queue.append(task)
    return queue


def make_task(name, priority=1):
    """Create a task dict."""
    return {"name": name, "priority": priority}


def process(queue):
    """Process all tasks in the queue and return results."""
    results = []
    for task in queue:
        results.append(f"done:{task['name']}")
    return results
````

# STEP 2.6 — Canonical Commitment Matching (CRITICAL)

You MUST compare extracted commitments against canonical bug-family commitments.

These represent NECESSARY conditions for a correct fix.

A commitment is considered CORRECT if it semantically matches ANY canonical commitment.

Do NOT require exact wording — use semantic equivalence.

---

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

---

# STEP 3 — Check Commitment Satisfaction

For each normalized commitment:

* SATISFIED → clearly implemented
* PARTIAL → partially implemented
* VIOLATED → not implemented or contradicted

Ground all judgments in code.

---

Use ONLY: CORRECT, PARTIAL, WRONG

Strict rules:

CORRECT = all required elements present
PARTIAL = some but not all
WRONG = none or incorrect

VAGUENESS RULE:
If commitment is not checkable → WRONG

ANTI-INFLATION RULE:
If reasoning applies to multiple bug types → not CORRECT

## Codebase (1 file)

### FILE 1/1: code_snippets_v2/mutable_default_a/queue.py ###
```python
"""Task queue with mutable default argument."""


def enqueue(task, queue=[]):
    """Add a task to the queue and return the queue.

    Invariant: each call with a single task (no explicit queue)
    must return a list containing only that task.
    """
    # BUG: default list is shared across calls — tasks accumulate
    queue.append(task)
    return queue


def make_task(name, priority=1):
    """Create a task dict."""
    return {"name": name, "priority": priority}


def process(queue):
    """Process all tasks in the queue and return results."""
    results = []
    for task in queue:
        results.append(f"done:{task['name']}")
    return results
```

Your goal is not just to describe the bug. Your goal is to make your diagnosis produce a concrete, checkable code fix.

## STEP 1 — Diagnose the mechanism

Bad:

- "this function has an issue"

- LOCAL: names a specific function, variable, or operation
- CHECKABLE: a reviewer could inspect the code and decide whether it is satisfied
- DIRECTLY IMPLIED by your diagnosis

<scope> must <action>

- "create_config must return a copy of DEFAULTS"
- "update_product must invalidate cache after write"
- "retry_send must stop retrying after success"

Do NOT write vague commitments like:
- "fix shared state"
- "improve correctness"
- "avoid bugs"

Describe the concrete code change you will make and why it satisfies the commitments.

Your fix strategy must:
- name the file/function you will change
- describe the exact operation you will add/remove/change
- explain why that change addresses the mechanism

## STEP 4 — Risk check (CRITICAL)

Before finalizing code, answer:

What is the most likely way this fix could still fail?

Rules:
- The risk must be specific and tied to the actual patch
- If the risk is real, REVISE your fix before writing final code
- If the risk is not real, explain specifically why the patch is safe
- Do NOT give a generic answer like "there may be edge cases"

Good:
- "This could still fail if update_product invalidates one cache layer but api reads from a separate local cache"
- "This is safe because create_config is the only constructor returning DEFAULTS and the patch replaces the direct shared reference"

Bad:
- "It might still fail in some cases"
- "There could be bugs elsewhere"
