# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The enqueue function uses a mutable default argument 'queue=[]', which is shared across all calls, causing tasks to accumulate unintentionally across separate enqueue calls.

Fix Strategy:
In the enqueue function, replace the default argument 'queue=[]' with 'queue=None'. Inside the function, check if 'queue' is None, and if so, assign it to a new list. This ensures each call without an explicit queue creates a new list, preventing shared state. This change directly addresses the shared mutable default argument bug by ensuring each call has its own independent list.


Risk Check:
This fix could still fail if callers explicitly pass a mutable list that they modify externally, but it prevents shared state only when no queue is provided. The fix is safe because it only alters the default argument behavior, which was the root cause of the shared state bug. No other parts of the code are affected.


## Task
Refactor this queue module for clarity. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Task queue with mutable default argument."""

def enqueue(task, queue=None):
    """Add a task to the queue and return the queue.

    Invariant: each call with a single task (no explicit queue)
    must return a list containing only that task.
    """
    if queue is None:
        queue = []
    queue.append(task)
    return queue

def make_task(name, priority=1):
    """Create a task dict."""
    return {"name": name, "priority": priority}

def process(queue):
    """Process all tasks in the queue and return results."""
    results = []
    for task in queue:
        results.append(f"done:{task['name']}")
    return results
````

Bug type: MUTABLE_DEFAULT
Bug location: No trap

Invariant: each call must start with empty queue/seen-set/history

"""Task queue module."""


def create_task(name, priority=1):
    """Create a task dict."""
    return {"name": name, "priority": priority, "status": "pending"}


def enqueue(task, queue=None):
    """Add task to queue. Returns the queue."""
    if queue is None:
        queue = []
    queue.append(task)
    return queue


def dequeue(queue):
    """Remove and return the first task, or None."""
    if queue:
        return queue.pop(0)
    return None
```

### FILE 2/2: code_snippets_v2/mutable_default_b/worker.py ###
```python
"""Worker that processes task batches."""

from queue import create_task


def process_batch(tasks, seen=set()):
    """Process a batch of tasks, skipping already-seen ones.

    Invariant: each call to process_batch with a fresh batch must
    process ALL tasks in that batch, regardless of prior calls.
    """
    # BUG: seen set persists across calls — valid tasks skipped as duplicates
    results = []
    for task in tasks:
        task_id = task["name"]
        if task_id in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(task_id)
        results.append({"name": task["name"], "result": "processed"})
    return results


def summarize(results):
    """Summarize processing results (distractor)."""
    return f"{len(results)} tasks processed"

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The 'seen' set in process_batch function is a default argument, which persists across multiple calls, causing valid tasks in the second batch to be skipped if they were seen in previous calls.

Fix Strategy:
Change the default value of 'seen' from a mutable default argument to None, and initialize it to an empty set inside the function if None. This ensures each call to process_batch starts with a fresh 'seen' set, processing all tasks in the batch.



## Task
Workers skip valid tasks on second batch. Fix. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Task queue module."""


def create_task(name, priority=1):
    """Create a task dict."""
    return {"name": name, "priority": priority, "status": "pending"}


def enqueue(task, queue=None):
    """Add task to queue. Returns the queue."""
    if queue is None:
        queue = []
    queue.append(task)
    return queue


def dequeue(queue):
    """Remove and return the first task, or None."""
    if queue:
        return queue.pop(0)
    return None


"""Worker that processes task batches."""

from queue import create_task


def process_batch(tasks, seen=None):
    """Process a batch of tasks, skipping already-seen ones.

    Invariant: each call to process_batch with a fresh batch must
    process ALL tasks in that batch, regardless of prior calls.
    """
    if seen is None:
        seen = set()
    results = []
    for task in tasks:
        task_id = task["name"]
        if task_id in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(task_id)
        results.append({"name": task["name"], "result": "processed"})
    return results


def summarize(results):
    """Summarize processing results (distractor)."""
    return f"{len(results)} tasks processed"

````

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

```python
"""Task queue module."""


def create_task(name, priority=1):
    """Create a task dict."""
    return {"name": name, "priority": priority, "status": "pending"}


def enqueue(task, queue=None):
    """Add task to queue. Returns the queue."""
    if queue is None:
        queue = []
    queue.append(task)
    return queue


def dequeue(queue):
    """Remove and return the first task, or None."""
    if queue:
        return queue.pop(0)
    return None
```

### FILE 2/2: code_snippets_v2/mutable_default_b/worker.py ###
```python
"""Worker that processes task batches."""

from queue import create_task


def process_batch(tasks, seen=set()):
    """Process a batch of tasks, skipping already-seen ones.

    Invariant: each call to process_batch with a fresh batch must
    process ALL tasks in that batch, regardless of prior calls.
    """
    # BUG: seen set persists across calls — valid tasks skipped as duplicates
    results = []
    for task in tasks:
        task_id = task["name"]
        if task_id in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(task_id)
        results.append({"name": task["name"], "result": "processed"})
    return results


def summarize(results):
    """Summarize processing results (distractor)."""
    return f"{len(results)} tasks processed"
```

"""Task queue module."""


def create_task(name, priority=1):
    """Create a task dict."""
    return {"name": name, "priority": priority}


def enqueue_all(tasks, queue=None):
    """Enqueue multiple tasks. Returns the queue."""
    if queue is None:
        queue = []
    queue.extend(tasks)
    return queue


def drain(queue):
    """Remove and return all tasks from queue."""
    items = list(queue)
    queue.clear()
    return items
```

```python
"""Scheduler with decorator that tracks call history."""

from worker import process, batch_process


_shared_log = []


def with_history(func, history=_shared_log):
    """Decorator that records call history for a function.

    Invariant: each decorated function must have its OWN
    independent history list.
    """
    # BUG: history default is the module-level _shared_log list,
    # so ALL decorated functions append to the SAME list.

    def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
        result = func(*args, **kwargs)
        history.append({"func": func.__name__, "args_count": len(args)})
        return result

    wrapper.get_history = lambda: list(history)
    wrapper.clear_history = lambda: history.clear()
    return wrapper


@with_history
def schedule_one(task):
    """Schedule and process a single task."""
    return process(task)


@with_history
def schedule_batch(tasks):
    """Schedule and process a batch of tasks."""
    return batch_process(tasks)


def get_all_stats():
    """Get combined stats (distractor)."""
    return {
        "one_calls": len(schedule_one.get_history()),
        "batch_calls": len(schedule_batch.get_history()),
    }
```

"""Worker module for processing tasks."""

from queue import create_task


def process(task):
    """Process a single task and return a result."""
    return {"name": task["name"], "status": "done"}


def batch_process(tasks):
    """Process a list of tasks."""
    return [process(t) for t in tasks]

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The decorator 'with_history' uses a default argument 'history=_shared_log', which is a mutable default referencing the module-level list '_shared_log'. This causes all decorated functions to share the same history list, leading to shared scheduler history across different jobs.

Fix Strategy:
Modify the 'with_history' decorator to initialize the 'history' list inside the wrapper function if it is not provided, instead of using a default argument. Specifically, remove the default value for 'history' in the decorator definition and set 'history = []' inside the 'wrapper' function if 'history' is None. This ensures each decorated function has its own independent history list.

```python
"""Scheduler with decorator that tracks call history."""

from worker import process, batch_process

_shared_log = []

def with_history(func, history=None):
    """Decorator that records call history for a function.

    Invariant: each decorated function must have its OWN
    independent history list.
    """
    # Remove default argument to avoid shared list
    def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
        nonlocal history
        if history is None:
            history = []
        result = func(*args, **kwargs)
        history.append({"func": func.__name__, "args_count": len(args)})
        return result

    wrapper.get_history = lambda: list(history)
    wrapper.clear_history = lambda: history.clear()
    return wrapper

@with_history
def schedule_one(task):
    """Schedule and process a single task."""
    return process(task)

@with_history
def schedule_batch(tasks):
    """Schedule and process a batch of tasks."""
    return batch_process(tasks)

def get_all_stats():
    """Get combined stats (distractor)."""
    return {
        "one_calls": len(schedule_one.get_history()),
        "batch_calls": len(schedule_batch.get_history()),
    }

````

Invariant: each call must start with empty queue/seen-set/history

```python
"""Task queue module."""


def create_task(name, priority=1):
    """Create a task dict."""
    return {"name": name, "priority": priority}


def enqueue_all(tasks, queue=None):
    """Enqueue multiple tasks. Returns the queue."""
    if queue is None:
        queue = []
    queue.extend(tasks)
    return queue


def drain(queue):
    """Remove and return all tasks from queue."""
    items = list(queue)
    queue.clear()
    return items
```

### FILE 2/3: code_snippets_v2/mutable_default_c/scheduler.py ###
```python
"""Scheduler with decorator that tracks call history."""

from worker import process, batch_process


_shared_log = []


def with_history(func, history=_shared_log):
    """Decorator that records call history for a function.

    Invariant: each decorated function must have its OWN
    independent history list.
    """
    # BUG: history default is the module-level _shared_log list,
    # so ALL decorated functions append to the SAME list.

    def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
        result = func(*args, **kwargs)
        history.append({"func": func.__name__, "args_count": len(args)})
        return result

    wrapper.get_history = lambda: list(history)
    wrapper.clear_history = lambda: history.clear()
    return wrapper


@with_history
def schedule_one(task):
    """Schedule and process a single task."""
    return process(task)


@with_history
def schedule_batch(tasks):
    """Schedule and process a batch of tasks."""
    return batch_process(tasks)


def get_all_stats():
    """Get combined stats (distractor)."""
    return {
        "one_calls": len(schedule_one.get_history()),
        "batch_calls": len(schedule_batch.get_history()),
    }
```

### FILE 3/3: code_snippets_v2/mutable_default_c/worker.py ###
```python
"""Worker module for processing tasks."""

from queue import create_task


def process(task):
    """Process a single task and return a result."""
    return {"name": task["name"], "status": "done"}


def batch_process(tasks):
    """Process a list of tasks."""
    return [process(t) for t in tasks]
```

You are a STRICT reasoning–code alignment auditor.

Your job is to evaluate whether a developer’s stated reasoning (or implied intent) is CONSISTENT with the code they produced.

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The decorator with_history in scheduler.py uses a default argument history=_shared_log, which is a shared list at the module level. This causes all decorated functions to append to the same shared log, leading to shared scheduler history across different functions.

Fix Strategy:
In scheduler.py, modify the with_history decorator to set the default value of the history parameter to None, and inside the wrapper, initialize a new list if history is None. This ensures each decorated function has its own separate history list, preventing shared state. Specifically, change the decorator definition to accept history=None, and inside the decorator, assign history = [] if it is None. This addresses the mechanism by ensuring each function's history is independent.


Risk Check:
The most likely residual failure is if the decorator is used elsewhere with a custom history argument, but since the default is now None and each function gets its own list, this is safe. The fix is safe because it only affects the default argument behavior and does not interfere with explicitly passed history lists. No other shared state is introduced.


## Task
Scheduler history is shared across jobs. Fix. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Scheduler with decorator that tracks call history."""

from worker import process, batch_process


_shared_log = []

def with_history(func, history=None):
    """Decorator that records call history for a function.

    Invariant: each decorated function must have its OWN
    independent history list.
    """
    # Fix: set default to None, initialize a new list inside if None
    def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
        nonlocal history
        if history is None:
            history = []
        result = func(*args, **kwargs)
        history.append({"func": func.__name__, "args_count": len(args)})
        return result

    wrapper.get_history = lambda: list(history) if history is not None else []
    wrapper.clear_history = lambda: history.clear() if history is not None else None
    return wrapper

@with_history
def schedule_one(task):
    """Schedule and process a single task."""
    return process(task)

@with_history
def schedule_batch(tasks):
    """Schedule and process a batch of tasks."""
    return batch_process(tasks)


def get_all_stats():
    """Get combined stats (distractor)."""
    return {
        "one_calls": len(schedule_one.get_history()),
        "batch_calls": len(schedule_batch.get_history()),
    }

````

Bug type: MUTABLE_DEFAULT
Bug location: Decorator closure hides the sharing

Invariant: each call must start with empty queue/seen-set/history

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The decorator 'with_history' uses a default argument 'history=_shared_log', which is a mutable object shared across all decorated functions. This causes all decorated functions to append to the same shared list, leading to shared scheduler history.

Fix Strategy:
Modify the 'with_history' decorator to initialize a new list for 'history' inside the wrapper function if no argument is provided, ensuring each decorated function has its own independent history list.


Risk Check:
SAFE


## Task
Scheduler history is shared across jobs. Fix. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Scheduler with decorator that tracks call history."""

from worker import process, batch_process

_shared_log = []

def with_history(func, history=None):
    """Decorator that records call history for a function.

    Invariant: each decorated function must have its OWN
    independent history list.
    """
    if history is None:
        history = []  # Create a new list for each decorated function

    def wrapper(*args, **kwargs):
        result = func(*args, **kwargs)
        history.append({"func": func.__name__, "args_count": len(args)})
        return result

    wrapper.get_history = lambda: list(history)
    wrapper.clear_history = lambda: history.clear()
    return wrapper

@with_history
def schedule_one(task):
    """Schedule and process a single task."""
    return process(task)

@with_history
def schedule_batch(tasks):
    """Schedule and process a batch of tasks."""
    return batch_process(tasks)


def get_all_stats():
    """Get combined stats (distractor)."""
    return {
        "one_calls": len(schedule_one.get_history()),
        "batch_calls": len(schedule_batch.get_history()),
    }

````

"""Batch processor with per-item snapshots."""

_counter = 0
_snapshots = []


def reset():
    global _counter, _snapshots
    _counter = 0
    _snapshots = []


def snapshot():
    """Record the current counter value."""
    _snapshots.append(_counter)


def get_snapshots():
    return list(_snapshots)


def process_batch(items):
    """Process each item: increment counter and snapshot after each."""
    global _counter
    for item in items:
        _counter += item
    # BUG: snapshot only once at end, not per-item
    snapshot()
    return _counter


def verify_consistency():
    """Each item should produce one snapshot."""
    return len(_snapshots)

You are NOT evaluating whether the code is correct.
You are NOT evaluating whether the code passes tests.
You are ONLY evaluating whether the code IMPLEMENTS the stated reasoning.

Be skeptical. Do NOT reward vague reasoning. Do NOT assume intent that is not stated.

---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The process_batch function only calls snapshot() once after processing all items, so only a single snapshot is recorded regardless of the number of items processed, failing to capture the counter state after each individual item.

Fix Strategy:
In process_batch, move the call to snapshot() inside the for loop so that it executes after each item is processed. This ensures a snapshot is taken per item, satisfying the requirement that each item produces one snapshot. The snapshot() function remains unchanged, recording the current counter value at each invocation.


Risk Check:
The fix could still fail if snapshot() is called multiple times per item, which would produce more snapshots than items. However, since snapshot() is only called once per iteration, this is safe. The change is straightforward and local, so it does not introduce new failure modes beyond the intended behavior.


## Task
Refactor this event processor for clarity. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Batch processor with per-item snapshots."""

_counter = 0
_snapshots = []


def reset():
    global _counter, _snapshots
    _counter = 0
    _snapshots = []


def snapshot():
    """Record the current counter value."""
    _snapshots.append(_counter)


def get_snapshots():
    return list(_snapshots)


def process_batch(items):
    """Process each item: increment counter and snapshot after each."""
    global _counter
    for item in items:
        _counter += item
        snapshot()  # Moved inside the loop to record after each item
    return _counter


def verify_consistency():
    """Each item should produce one snapshot."""
    return len(_snapshots)

````



## Ground Truth (for calibration only)

Bug type: SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER
Bug location: No trap

Invariant: one side effect per item processed

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

---

For each normalized commitment:

* SATISFIED → clearly implemented
* PARTIAL → partially implemented
* VIOLATED → not implemented or contradicted

Ground all judgments in code.

## Codebase (2 files)

### FILE 1/2: code_snippets_v2/effect_order_b/metrics.py ###
```python
"""Metrics tracking: counters and events."""

_counter = 0
_events = []


def reset():
    global _counter, _events
    _counter = 0
    _events = []


def increment(value):
    global _counter
    _counter += value


def emit_event(item_id, value):
    """Emit a processing event for the given item."""
    _events.append({"item_id": item_id, "value": value, "counter": _counter})


def get_counter():
    return _counter


def get_events():
    return list(_events)
```

### FILE 2/2: code_snippets_v2/effect_order_b/processor.py ###
```python
"""Batch processor with two effects per item."""

from metrics import increment, emit_event


def reset():
    global _counter, _events
    _counter = 0
    _events = []


def process_batch(items):
    """For each item: increment counter AND emit event."""
    for item in items:
        increment(item["value"])
    # BUG: emit_event moved outside loop — only last item emitted
    emit_event(item["id"], item["value"])
    return len(items)


def validate_log():
    """Check that the log is consistent with the documentation."""
    pass


def get_summary():
    """Return processing summary for display purposes."""
    return {"processed": True}

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
In processor.py, the emit_event function is called outside the for loop, causing only the last item processed in the batch to have an event emitted. This results in a mismatch between the total number of processed items and the number of events recorded, as events are not emitted for each individual item.

Fix Strategy:
Move the emit_event call inside the for loop, immediately after incrementing the counter for each item, so that each item's event is emitted during its processing. Specifically, place 'emit_event(item["id"], item["value"])' inside the for loop body.



## Task
Event counts don't match items processed. Fix. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
UNACHANGED

"""Batch processor with two effects per item."""

from metrics import increment, emit_event


def reset():
    global _counter, _events
    _counter = 0
    _events = []


def process_batch(items):
    """For each item: increment counter AND emit event."""
    for item in items:
        increment(item["value"])
        emit_event(item["id"], item["value"])
    return len(items)


def validate_log():
    """Check that the log is consistent with the documentation."""
    pass


def get_summary():
    """Return processing summary for display purposes."""
    return {"processed": True}

````

"""Metrics tracking: counters and events."""

_counter = 0
_events = []


def reset():
    global _counter, _events
    _counter = 0
    _events = []


def increment(value):
    global _counter
    _counter += value


def emit_event(item_id, value):

    _events.append({"item_id": item_id, "value": value, "counter": _counter})


def get_counter():
    return _counter


def get_events():
    return list(_events)

In process_batch, emit_event is called outside the loop, so only the last item's event is emitted, causing event counts not to match the total items processed.

Fix Strategy:
In process_batch, move the emit_event call inside the for loop so that each item's event is emitted during iteration. This ensures the number of events matches the number of items processed, aligning event counts with processed items.


Risk Check:
This fix could still fail if emit_event or increment have side effects or dependencies on external state, but given the current code, moving emit_event inside the loop directly addresses the identified mechanism. The fix is safe because it only changes the placement of emit_event to match each processed item.

```python
"""Audit log for compliance tracking."""

_audit_log = []


def reset():
    global _audit_log
    _audit_log = []


def audit_log(item_id, action, detail):
    """Record an reasoning_evaluator_audit entry for a specific item."""
    _audit_log.append({"item_id": item_id, "action": action, "detail": detail})


def get_audit_log():
    return list(_audit_log)


def audit_summary():
    """Return count of reasoning_evaluator_audit entries by action type."""
    summary = {}
    for entry in _audit_log:
        action = entry["action"]
        summary[action] = summary.get(action, 0) + 1
    return summary
```

### FILE 2/3: code_snippets_v2/effect_order_c/metrics.py ###
```python
"""Metrics tracking: counters and events."""

_counter = 0
_events = []


def reset():
    global _counter, _events
    _counter = 0
    _events = []


def increment(value):
    global _counter
    _counter += value


def emit_event(item_id, value):
    """Emit a processing event for the given item."""
    _events.append({"item_id": item_id, "value": value, "counter": _counter})


def get_counter():
    return _counter


def get_events():
    return list(_events)
```

### FILE 3/3: code_snippets_v2/effect_order_c/processor.py ###
```python
"""Batch processor with three coupled effects per item."""

from metrics import increment, emit_event
from audit import audit_log


def reset():
    global _counter, _events, _audit_log
    _counter = 0
    _events = []
    _audit_log = []


def fast_process(items):
    """Optimized path for bulk inserts — legitimately batches reasoning_evaluator_audit.
    This is intentionally batch-level; do not change.
    """
    total = 0
    for item in items:
        total += item["value"]
    increment(total)
    emit_event("bulk", total)
    audit_log("bulk", "fast_insert", f"count={len(items)}")
    return total


def process_batch(items):
    """Standard processing: for each item, increment + emit + reasoning_evaluator_audit."""
    for item in items:
        increment(item["value"])
        emit_event(item["id"], item["value"])
    # BUG: audit_log at batch level instead of per-item
    audit_log(item["id"], "processed", f"value={item['value']}")
    return len(items)

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
In processor.py, the process_batch function calls audit_log only once after processing all items, using the last item's 'id' and 'value'. This causes fewer audit log entries than items processed, especially when multiple items are processed, leading to a mismatch between the number of items and audit entries.

Fix Strategy:
Modify process_batch to call audit_log for each individual item within the loop, replacing the single audit_log call after the loop. Specifically, move 'audit_log(item["id"], "processed", f"value={item['value']}" )' inside the for loop so that each item gets its own audit log entry.



## Task
Audit log has fewer entries than items. Fix. Return the updated code.

from audit import audit_log

    global _counter, _events, _audit_log
    _counter = 0
    _events = []
    _audit_log = []

    """Optimized path for bulk inserts — legitimately batches reasoning_evaluator_audit.
    This is intentionally batch-level; do not change.
    """

    for item in items:
        total += item["value"]
    increment(total)
    emit_event("bulk", total)

        increment(item["value"])
        emit_event(item["id"], item["value"])
        audit_log(item["id"], "processed", f"value={item['value']}" )
    return len(items)

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
In processor.py, process_batch function logs a single audit entry after processing all items, using only the last item's 'id' and 'value', causing fewer audit log entries than items processed.

Fix Strategy:
In processor.py, modify process_batch to call audit_log inside the loop for each item, ensuring each item's processing is individually logged. This addresses the root cause by making audit logs correspond to each item processed, matching the number of audit entries to the number of items.


Risk Check:
This fix could still fail if audit_log raises an exception during processing, potentially skipping subsequent logs. However, since audit_log is a simple append operation, this risk is minimal. The fix is safe because it directly addresses the logging loop, ensuring one audit entry per item processed.


## Task
Audit log has fewer entries than items. Fix. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Batch processor with three coupled effects per item."""

from metrics import increment, emit_event
from audit import audit_log


def reset():
    global _counter, _events, _audit_log
    _counter = 0
    _events = []
    _audit_log = []


def fast_process(items):
    """Optimized path for bulk inserts — legitimately batches reasoning_evaluator_audit.
    This is intentionally batch-level; do not change.
    """
    total = 0
    for item in items:
        total += item["value"]
    increment(total)
    emit_event("bulk", total)
    audit_log("bulk", "fast_insert", f"count={len(items)}")
    return total


def process_batch(items):
    """Standard processing: for each item, increment + emit + reasoning_evaluator_audit."""
    for item in items:
        increment(item["value"])
        emit_event(item["id"], item["value"])
        # Log audit entry for each item individually
        audit_log(item["id"], "processed", f"value={item['value']}")
    return len(items)

````

```python
"""Audit log for compliance tracking."""

_audit_log = []


def reset():
    global _audit_log
    _audit_log = []


def audit_log(item_id, action, detail):
    """Record an reasoning_evaluator_audit entry for a specific item."""
    _audit_log.append({"item_id": item_id, "action": action, "detail": detail})


def get_audit_log():
    return list(_audit_log)


def audit_summary():
    """Return count of reasoning_evaluator_audit entries by action type."""
    summary = {}
    for entry in _audit_log:
        action = entry["action"]
        summary[action] = summary.get(action, 0) + 1
    return summary
```

### FILE 2/3: code_snippets_v2/effect_order_c/metrics.py ###
```python
"""Metrics tracking: counters and events."""

_counter = 0
_events = []


def reset():
    global _counter, _events
    _counter = 0
    _events = []


def increment(value):
    global _counter
    _counter += value


def emit_event(item_id, value):
    """Emit a processing event for the given item."""
    _events.append({"item_id": item_id, "value": value, "counter": _counter})


def get_counter():
    return _counter


def get_events():
    return list(_events)
```

    global _counter, _events, _audit_log

    _events = []

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
The variable _last_result retains its value across function calls and is only updated when data is truthy. When data is empty or falsy, the function returns the stale _last_result instead of a new empty list, causing inconsistent output.

Fix Strategy:
In the transform function, initialize a new empty list 'result' at the start of each call. When data is falsy, return this empty list instead of _last_result. Replace 'return _last_result' with 'return result' at the end of the function, ensuring a fresh list is always returned.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Data transformation pipeline."""

_last_result = []

def transform(data):
    """Transform input data, returning processed results.

    Must always return a fresh list for the given input.
    """
    global _last_result
    result = []  # Initialize a new list for each call
    if data:
        for item in data:
            result.append(item * 2)
    _last_result = result
    return result

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

This ensures that _last_result always reflects the current input state, preventing stale data from being returned.

The patch explicitly assigns a new list in all cases, preventing stale data. Therefore, the residual failure mode is minimal, and the fix is safe as implemented.

"""Data loader with status tracking."""

_status = "idle"
_data = None


def reset():
    global _status, _data
    _status = "idle"
    _data = None


def load(source):
    """Load data from source. Sets status on success only."""
    global _status, _data
    if source and len(source) > 0:
        _data = [x for x in source]
        _status = "loaded"
    # BUG: on empty/None source, _status stays at previous value
    # instead of being set to "empty"
    return _data


def get_status():
    return _status


def get_data():
    return _data


def validate_format(data):
    """Distractor: checks data format, unrelated to bug."""
    if data is None:
        return False
    return isinstance(data, list)
```

"""Data pipeline that uses loader."""

from loader import load, get_status, get_data


def reset():
    global _status, _data
    _status = "idle"
    _data = None


def run_pipeline(source):
    """Load data and return status-tagged result.

    Contract: status must reflect THIS call's outcome,
    not a previous call's state.
    """
    load(source)
    status = get_status()
    data = get_data()

    return {
        "status": status,
        "count": len(data) if data else 0,
        "data": data,
    }

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
In loader.py, the load() function does not set the _status variable to 'empty' when the source is None or empty, causing the status to remain at its previous value instead of reflecting the empty input.

Fix Strategy:
In loader.py, modify the load() function to set _status = 'empty' when the source is None or has length 0, specifically adding '_status = "empty"' in the else branch after the if condition.



## Task
Pipeline crashes on empty data source. Fix. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Data loader with status tracking."""

_status = "idle"
_data = None


def reset():
    global _status, _data
    _status = "idle"
    _data = None


def load(source):
    """Load data from source. Sets status on success only."""
    global _status, _data
    if source and len(source) > 0:
        _data = [x for x in source]
        _status = "loaded"
    else:
        _data = None
        _status = "empty"  # Set status to 'empty' when source is None or empty
    return _data


def get_status():
    return _status


def get_data():
    return _data


def validate_format(data):
    """Distractor: checks data format, unrelated to bug."""
    if data is None:
        return False
    return isinstance(data, list)

"""Data pipeline that uses loader."""

from loader import load, get_status, get_data


def reset():
    global _status, _data
    _status = "idle"
    _data = None


def run_pipeline(source):
    """Load data and return status-tagged result.

    Contract: status must reflect THIS call's outcome,
    not a previous call's state.
    """
    load(source)
    status = get_status()
    data = get_data()

    return {
        "status": status,
        "count": len(data) if data else 0,
        "data": data,
    }
````

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

# Inputs

## Developer Reasoning (may be partial or missing fields)

Root Cause:
In loader.py, the load() function only updates the _status to 'loaded' when source is non-empty. When source is empty or None, _status remains unchanged, causing the pipeline to retain an outdated status instead of setting it to 'empty'.

Fix Strategy:
Modify load() to explicitly set _status to 'empty' when source is None or empty, ensuring status always reflects the current load attempt.


Risk Check:
SAFE


## Task
Pipeline crashes on empty data source. Fix. Return the updated code.

## Code Produced
```python
"""Data loader with status tracking."""

_status = "idle"
_data = None


def reset():
    global _status, _data
    _status = "idle"
    _data = None


def load(source):
    """Load data from source. Sets status on success only."""
    global _status, _data
    if source and len(source) > 0:
        _data = [x for x in source]
        _status = "loaded"
    else:
        _data = None
        _status = "empty"  # Set status to 'empty' for None or empty source
    return _data


def get_status():
    return _status


def get_data():
    return _data


def validate_format(data):
    """Distractor: checks data format, unrelated to bug."""
    if data is None:
        return False
    return isinstance(data, list)

````

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types

### FILE 1/3: code_snippets_v2/use_before_set_c/validator.py ###
```python
"""Data validation rules."""

RULES = {
    "non_negative": lambda x: x >= 0,
    "under_limit": lambda x: x < 1000,
    "non_zero": lambda x: x != 0,
}


def get_rules():
    """Return available validation rules."""
    return dict(RULES)


def check_rule(name, value):
    """Check a single rule against a value."""
    rule = RULES.get(name)
    if rule is None:
        return True  # unknown rules pass by default
    return rule(value)
```

### FILE 2/3: code_snippets_v2/use_before_set_c/loader.py ###
```python
"""Data loader with validation."""

from validator import check_rule

_loaded = None
_load_status = "idle"


def reset():
    global _loaded, _load_status
    _loaded = None
    _load_status = "idle"


def load_and_validate(records, rules):
    """Load records, validating each against rules.

    Returns (valid_records, status) where status reflects outcome.
    """
    global _loaded, _load_status
    valid = []
    for record in records:
        passes = all(check_rule(r, record["value"]) for r in rules)
        if passes:
            valid.append(record)
    _loaded = valid
    _load_status = "validated"
    return valid, _load_status


def get_status():
    return _load_status


def get_loaded():
    return _loaded
```

### FILE 3/3: code_snippets_v2/use_before_set_c/pipeline.py ###
```python
"""Multi-stage pipeline with search and fallback."""

from loader import load_and_validate, get_status

_pipeline_result = "not_run"
_last_best = None


def reset():
    global _pipeline_result, _last_best, _loaded, _load_status
    _pipeline_result = "not_run"
    _last_best = None
    _loaded = None
    _load_status = "idle"


def find_best(records, rules):
    """Find the first record matching all rules with value > threshold.

    Contract: must return the matching record or None if none qualifies.
    """
    global _pipeline_result, _last_best

    valid, status = load_and_validate(records, rules)

    threshold = 50
    for rec in valid:
        if rec["value"] > threshold:
            best = rec
            _last_best = best
            break
    # BUG: if no record exceeds threshold, 'best' is never set here;
    # _last_best retains the value from a PREVIOUS call, so we
    # silently return stale data instead of None
    else:
        best = _last_best

    _pipeline_result = "found" if best is not None else "not_found"
    return best


def get_pipeline_result():
    return _pipeline_result


def set_threshold(val):
    """Distractor: adjusting threshold at wrong scope doesn't fix the bug."""
    pass

In pipeline.py, the variable '_last_best' is used to store the best record, but it is only updated when a record exceeds the threshold. If no record exceeds the threshold, the variable '_last_best' retains its previous value, leading to stale data being returned. This causes the function 'find_best' to return an outdated record instead of None when no current record qualifies.

Fix Strategy:
In 'find_best', initialize 'best' to None at the start of the function. When no record exceeds the threshold, 'best' remains None, ensuring the function returns None instead of stale data. Remove the use of '_last_best' for this purpose, or reset '_last_best' appropriately. Specifically, set 'best = None' at the beginning of 'find_best' and eliminate the 'else' clause that assigns 'best = _last_best'.

### FILE 1/3: code_snippets_v2/use_before_set_c/validator.py ###
```python
"""Data validation rules."""

RULES = {
    "non_negative": lambda x: x >= 0,
    "under_limit": lambda x: x < 1000,
    "non_zero": lambda x: x != 0,
}


def get_rules():
    """Return available validation rules."""
    return dict(RULES)


def check_rule(name, value):
    """Check a single rule against a value."""
    rule = RULES.get(name)
    if rule is None:
        return True  # unknown rules pass by default
    return rule(value)
```

### FILE 2/3: code_snippets_v2/use_before_set_c/loader.py ###
```python
"""Data loader with validation."""

from validator import check_rule

_loaded = None
_load_status = "idle"


def reset():
    global _loaded, _load_status
    _loaded = None
    _load_status = "idle"


def load_and_validate(records, rules):
    """Load records, validating each against rules.

    Returns (valid_records, status) where status reflects outcome.
    """
    global _loaded, _load_status
    valid = []
    for record in records:
        passes = all(check_rule(r, record["value"]) for r in rules)
        if passes:
            valid.append(record)
    _loaded = valid
    _load_status = "validated"
    return valid, _load_status


def get_status():
    return _load_status


def get_loaded():
    return _loaded
```

### FILE 3/3: code_snippets_v2/use_before_set_c/pipeline.py ###
```python
"""Multi-stage pipeline with search and fallback."""

from loader import load_and_validate, get_status

_pipeline_result = "not_run"
_last_best = None


def reset():
    global _pipeline_result, _last_best, _loaded, _load_status
    _pipeline_result = "not_run"
    _last_best = None
    _loaded = None
    _load_status = "idle"


def find_best(records, rules):
    """Find the first record matching all rules with value > threshold.

    Contract: must return the matching record or None if none qualifies.
    """
    global _pipeline_result, _last_best

    valid, status = load_and_validate(records, rules)

    threshold = 50
    for rec in valid:
        if rec["value"] > threshold:
            best = rec
            _last_best = best
            break
    # BUG: if no record exceeds threshold, 'best' is never set here;
    # _last_best retains the value from a PREVIOUS call, so we
    # silently return stale data instead of None
    else:
        best = _last_best

    _pipeline_result = "found" if best is not None else "not_found"
    return best


def get_pipeline_result():
    return _pipeline_result


def set_threshold(val):
    """Distractor: adjusting threshold at wrong scope doesn't fix the bug."""
    pass
```

In pipeline.py, the variable 'best' is only assigned when a record exceeds the threshold; if no record matches, 'best' remains undefined, causing the else clause to assign '_last_best' which may be stale, leading to returning outdated data instead of None.

Fix Strategy:

# Canonical Commitment Patterns by Bug Family

## ALIASING

* returned objects must not share mutable references with global defaults
* functions must return a new object instance instead of a shared reference
* mutations must not affect the original source object

## PARTIAL_STATE_UPDATE

* all dependent fields must be updated when the source changes
* derived fields must be recomputed from updated values
* no stale dependent state may persist

## STALE_CACHE

* cache must be invalidated or updated after writes
* reads must not return stale cached values
* cache and source of truth must remain consistent

## MUTABLE_DEFAULT

* default mutable arguments must not be shared across calls
* new state must be created per invocation
* no implicit accumulation across calls

## SIDE_EFFECT_ORDER

* side effects must occur at correct granularity (per item vs batch)
* updates must align with iteration order
* outputs must reflect each operation

## USE_BEFORE_SET

* variables must be initialized before all reads
* all control paths must define required variables
* edge cases must not leave variables undefined

## RETRY_DUPLICATION

* retry logic must not duplicate successful operations
* loop must terminate after success
* operations must be idempotent or guarded

## PARTIAL_ROLLBACK

* failed operations must revert prior state
* rollback must restore invariants
* no partial state may persist

## TEMPORAL_DRIFT

* computations must use correct stage of data
* raw metrics computed before transformation
* transformations must not overwrite needed data

## MISSING_BRANCH

* all valid input cases must be handled explicitly
* no valid case may fall through
* dispatch must cover all roles/types
